I just feel like a failure who can't stop myself from getting bigger.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of overeating and trying to get rid of the food happen, and for how many months have you been experiencing this?\n\nPatient: This has been going on for about seven months, ever since my husband's health worsened and my doctor started pushing me about my weight. It happens at least once or twice a week, usually on the stressful days when I am exhausted from caregiving.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific target weight that you feel you absolutely must achieve, and is this target weight significantly below what would be considered a healthy weight for you?\n\nPatient: I don't really have a specific target weight in mind, and I'm certainly not trying to become underweight. I'm just incredibly distressed by how big I feel and failing my doctor's expectations about my BMI, and I just want to stop myself from getting any bigger.\n\nDoctor: When you have these episodes of overeating, do you feel a complete loss of control over your eating, and what methods do you use to try to 'get rid of' the food?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's like a switch flips and I go into a trance, unable to stop my hands from reaching for more food even when I'm uncomfortably full. Afterward, out of sheer panic, I go into the bathroom, turn on the fan so my husband won't hear, and make myself throw up to try and erase what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Besides making yourself throw up, do you ever use other methods to prevent weight gain, such as taking laxatives, diet pills, or fasting for long periods?\n\nPatient: No, I have never used laxatives, diet pills, or tried to fast. Making myself throw up is the only thing I've resorted to, even though I feel deeply ashamed of it every single time."

It happens at least once or twice a week, usually on the stressful days when I am exhausted from caregiving.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific target weight that you feel you absolutely must achieve, and is this target weight significantly below what would be considered a healthy weight for you?\n\nPatient: I don't really have a specific target weight in mind, and I'm certainly not trying to become underweight. I'm just incredibly distressed by how big I feel and failing my doctor's expectations about my BMI, and I just want to stop myself from getting any bigger.\n\nDoctor: When you have these episodes of overeating, do you feel a complete loss of control over your eating, and what methods do you use to try to 'get rid of' the food?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's like a switch flips and I go into a trance, unable to stop my hands from reaching for more food even when I'm uncomfortably full. Afterward, out of sheer panic, I go into the bathroom, turn on the fan so my husband won't hear, and make myself throw up to try and erase what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Besides making yourself throw up, do you ever use other methods to prevent weight gain, such as taking laxatives, diet pills, or fasting for long periods?\n\nPatient: No, I have never used laxatives, diet pills, or tried to fast. Making myself throw up is the only thing I've resorted to, even though I feel deeply ashamed of it every single time.\n\nDoctor: How much do your weight and body shape influence how you judge your self-worth as a person? Does it completely dominate how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, it completely dominates how I feel about myself, and I feel so ashamed of it. She finally agreed to see a mental health clinician after her primary care physician noticed unexplained fluctuations in her electrolytes and dental wear at a check‑up, gently raised concerns about her eating patterns, and suggested that the distress she described about her behavior wasn’t \"just stress\" and might benefit from specialized help.\n- Chief Complaint: I keep trying to get rid of what I eat after I overdo it, and I feel like I can’t stop doing it on my own.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S046 (Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance): Ever since my doctor started talking about my BMI, I can’t look in a mirror without zeroing in on my stomach and arms and thinking, ‘You’re a failure, you can’t even keep yourself from getting bigger,’ no matter what anyone else tells me about how I look or how much I do for my husband and the church.\n- S048 (Compensatory_Behavior_Purging): After I’ve stuffed myself, I go into the bathroom, turn the fan on so my husband can’t hear, and make myself throw up because it feels like the only way to erase what I just did and calm the panic about the calories, even though I’m ashamed and promise myself every time it’ll be the last.\n- S049 (Eating_Large_Amount): On the nights when my husband’s been especially sick and I’m worn out, I’ll stand in the kitchen and go through half a pan of brownies, a couple of dinner rolls with butter, and whatever leftovers are in the fridge in one sitting, way more than I’d ever eat if anyone else were around.\n- S050 (Loss_of_Control): Once I start picking at something, it’s like a switch flips and I keep eating past feeling full, telling myself to stop but my hands just keep reaching for more until I’m uncomfortably stuffed and almost in a trance.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- binge_purge_frequency: For at least the past several months, it’s been the same pattern where at least once or twice a week, usually after a really hard day with my husband’s care, I end up having one of these big eating episodes and then forcing myself to throw up afterward.\n- functional_impairment: I still show up for work and church, but I’ve started turning down evening visits with friends and rushing home after events because I’m either planning to binge and purge or recovering from it, and it leaves me exhausted and distracted when I’m supposed to be focusing on my husband or customers.\n- duration: This cycle of overeating in secret and then trying to get rid of it has been going on for about seven months, ever since my husband’s heart troubles got worse and my doctor started pushing me about my weight.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. I just feel like a failure who can't stop myself from getting bigger.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of overeating and trying to get rid of the food happen, and for how many months have you been experiencing this?\n\nPatient: This has been going on for about seven months, ever since my husband's health worsened and my doctor started pushing me about my weight. It happens at least once or twice a week, usually on the stressful days when I am exhausted from caregiving.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific target weight that you feel you absolutely must achieve, and is this target weight significantly below what would be considered a healthy weight for you?\n\nPatient: I don't really have a specific target weight in mind, and I'm certainly not trying to become underweight. I'm just incredibly distressed by how big I feel and failing my doctor's expectations about my BMI, and I just want to stop myself from getting any bigger.\n\nDoctor: When you have these episodes of overeating, do you feel a complete loss of control over your eating, and what methods do you use to try to 'get rid of' the food?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's like a switch flips and I go into a trance, unable to stop my hands from reaching for more food even when I'm uncomfortably full. Afterward, out of sheer panic, I go into the bathroom, turn on the fan so my husband won't hear, and make myself throw up to try and erase what I've done.\n\nDoctor: Besides making yourself throw up, do you ever use other methods to prevent weight gain, such as taking laxatives, diet pills, or fasting for long periods?\n\nPatient: No, I have never used laxatives, diet pills, or tried to fast. Making myself throw up is the only thing I've resorted to, even though I feel deeply ashamed of it every single time.\n\nDoctor: How much do your weight and body shape influence how you judge your self-worth as a person? Does it completely dominate how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, it completely dominates how I feel about myself, and I feel so ashamed of it. Ever since my doctor brought up my BMI, I can't look in a mirror without focusing on my stomach and arms and thinking I'm a complete failure. No matter what good things I do for my husband or my church, these negative thoughts about my body completely take over my self-worth.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned this happens at least once or twice a week. Over the last three months, has this consistently occurred at least twice every single week, or are there some weeks where it only happens once?\n\nPatient: It definitely varies depending on how stressful things are with my husband's care, so there are some weeks where it only happens once. However, it has consistently happened at least once or twice every single week for the past several months.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes of overeating, can you describe the amount of food you typically consume? Would you say it is a much larger amount of food than most people would eat in a similar short period?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is definitely a much larger amount than normal. On stressful nights when I'm alone in the kitchen, I'll eat half a pan of brownies, several dinner rolls with butter, and whatever leftovers are in the fridge in one sitting. Once I start, it's like a switch flips and I just can't stop myself.\n\nDoctor: On average, over the past three months, would you say these episodes of overeating followed by throwing up occurred at least twice a week, or has the average been less than that?"
  }
